**Python** is a [high-level](https://jbo.wikipedia.org/wiki/paiton "paiton – Lojban"), [general-purpose programming language](https://ka.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E1%83%9E%E1%83%90%E1%83%98%E1%83%97%E1%83%9D%E1%83%9C%E1%83%98_(%E1%83%9E%E1%83%A0%E1%83%9D%E1%83%92%E1%83%A0%E1%83%90%E1%83%9B%E1%83%98%E1%83%A0%E1%83%94%E1%83%91%E1%83%98%E1%83%A1_%E1%83%94%E1%83%9C%E1%83%90) "პაითონი (პროგრამირების ენა) – Georgian") that emphasizes [code readability](ka "პაითონი (პროგრამირების ენა) – Georgian"), simplicity, and ease-of-writing with the use of [significant indentation](ka "პაითონი (პროგრამირების ენა) –
Georgian"),[[38]](https://kaa.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmalast%C4%B1r%C4%B1w_tili) "Python (programmalastırıw tili) – Kara-Kalpak")
an extensive ("batteries-included") [standard library](https://kk.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Kazakh"), and [garbage collection](https://kk.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Kazakh"). Python supports multiple [programming paradigms](https://km.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E1%9E%95%E1%9E%B6%E1%9E%99%E1%9E%90%E1%9E%BB%E1%9E%93 "ផាយថុន – Khmer") but with an emphasis on [object-oriented programming](https://km.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E1%9E%95%E1%9E%B6%E1%9E%99%E1%9E%90%E1%9E%BB%E1%9E%93 "ផាយថុន – Khmer") and [dynamic typing](km "ផាយថុន").

[Guido van Rossum](https://ko.wikipedia.org/wiki/%ED%8C%8C%EC%9D%B4%EC%8D%AC "파이썬 – Korean") began working on Python in the late 1980s as a successor to the [ABC](ko "파이썬") programming language.
Python 3.0, released in 2008, was a major revision and not completely [backward-compatible](ks "پایتھَن (پرٛوگرامِنٛگ زَبان) – Kashmiri") with earlier versions. Beginning with Python
3.5,[[39]](https://ku.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(ziman%C3%AA_bernamesaziy%C3%AA) "Python (zimanê bernamesaziyê) – Kurdish")
capabilities and keywords for typing were added to the language, allowing optional
[static typing](https://ky.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Kyrgyz").[[40]](https://la.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(lingua_programmationis) "Python (lingua programmationis) – Latin")
As of 2026[[update]](https://lo.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E0%BB%84%E0%BA%9E%E0%BA%97%E0%BA%AD%E0%BA%99_(%E0%BA%9E%E0%BA%B2%E0%BA%AA%E0%BA%B2%E0%BB%82%E0%BA%9B%E0%BA%A3%E0%BB%81%E0%BA%81%E0%BA%A3%E0%BA%A1) "Python")[Python Software Foundation](https://lt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Lithuanian") supports Python 3.10, 3.11, 3.12, 3.13, and 3.14, following the project's annual release cycle and five-year support policy. Python 3.15.0rc1 (which defaults to [UTF-8](https://lv.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programm%C4%93%C5%A1anas_valoda) "Python (programmēšanas valoda) – Latvian")) is out in preview, and the stable release is expected to launch in October
2026.[[41]](mk "Пајтон (програмски јазик) – Macedonian")
Earlier versions in the 3.x series have reached end-of-life and no longer receive
security updates.

Python is widely taught as an introductory programming language.[[42]](ml "പൈത്തൺ (പ്രോഗ്രാമിങ്ങ് ഭാഷ) – Malayalam")


## History

Main article: [History of Python](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ext.globalCssJs.user.styles")[![application/atom+xml](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=startup&only=scripts&raw=1&skin=vector-2022 "ext.globalCssJs.user.styles")](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ext.globalCssJs.user.styles")
The designer of Python, [Guido van Rossum](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ext.globalCssJs.user.styles"), at [PyCon](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ext.globalCssJs.user.styles") US 2024
Python was conceived in the late 1980s[[11]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia")
by [Guido van Rossum](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") at [Centrum Wiskunde & Informatica](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") (CWI) in the [Netherlands](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) -
Wikipedia").[[43]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia")
It was designed as a successor to the [ABC](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") programming language, which was inspired by [SETL](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) -
Wikipedia"),[[44]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia")
capable of [exception handling](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") and interfacing with the [Amoeba](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") operating
system.[[18]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia")
Python implementation began in December 1989.[[43]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia")
Van Rossum first released it in 1991 as Python 0.9.0.[[43]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia")
Van Rossum assumed sole responsibility for the project, as the lead developer, until
12 July 2018, when he announced his "permanent vacation" from responsibilities as
Python's "[benevolent dictator for life](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia")" (BDFL); this title was bestowed on him by the Python community to reflect his long-term commitment as the project's chief
decision-maker.[[45]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) -
Wikipedia")[[c]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia")
In January 2019, active Python core developers elected a five-member Steering
Council to lead the project.[[46]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) -
Wikipedia")[[47]](//upload.wikimedia.org "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia")

The name *Python* derives from the British comedy series *[Monty Python's Flying Circus](//upload.wikimedia.org "Python (programming language) -
Wikipedia")*.[[48]](/w/rest.php/v1/search "Wikipedia (en)") (See [§ Naming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programming_language) "Wikipedia Atom feed").)

Python 2.0 was released on 16 October 2000, featuring many new features such as [list comprehensions](#bodyContent "></script></head>
<body class="), [cycle-detecting](#bodyContent "></script></head>
<body class=") garbage collection, [reference counting](#bodyContent "></script></head>
<body class="), and [Unicode](#bodyContent "></script></head>
<body class=") support.[[49]](#bodyContent "></script></head>
<body class=") Python 2.7's [end-of-life](#bodyContent "></script></head>
<body class=") was initially set for 2015, and then postponed to 2020 out of concern that a large body of existing code could not easily be forward-ported to
Python 3.[[50]](#bodyContent "Main menu")[[51]](/wiki/Main_Page "Main menu")
It no longer receives security patches or updates.[[52]](/wiki/Main_Page "Visit the main page
[z]")[[53]](/wiki/Main_Page "Visit the main page [z]") While Python 2.7 and older
versions are officially unsupported, a different unofficial Python implementation,
[PyPy](/wiki/Main_Page "Visit the main page [z]"), continues to support Python 2, i.e., "2.7.18+" (plus
3.11), with the plus signifying (at least some) "[backported](/wiki/Main_Page "Visit the main page [z]") security
updates".[[54]](/wiki/Main_Page "Visit the main page [z]")

Python 3.0 was released on 3 December 2008, and was a major revision and not
completely [backward-compatible](/wiki/Wikipedia:Contents "Visit the main page [z]") with earlier versions, with some new semantics and changed syntax.
Python 2.7.18, released in 2020, was the last release of Python 2.[[55]](//en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Contact_us "Learn about Wikipedia and how it works")
Several releases in the Python 3.x series have added new syntax to the language,
and made a few (considered very minor) backward-incompatible changes.

As of August 2026[[update]](/wiki/Wikipedia:Community_portal "The hub for editors")
is the latest stable release, and since 3.14 official Android binary releases are
available. All older 3.x versions had a security update down to Python 3.9.24 then
again with 3.9.25, the final version in 3.9 series; and then again security updates
in August 2026 down to 3.10.21. Python 3.10 is, since November 2025, the oldest supported
branch.[[56]](/wiki/Special:Search "Search Wikipedia [f]") Python 3.15 has release
candidate 1 out. Releases receive two years of full support followed by three years
of security support.


## Design philosophy and features

Python is a [multi-paradigm programming language](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en "Search Wikipedia [f]"). Object-oriented programming and structured programming are fully supported, and many of their features support functional programming and [aspect-oriented programming](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en "Search Wikipedia [f]") – including [metaprogramming](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en "Search Wikipedia
[f]")[[57]](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en "Change the appearance of the page's font size, width, and color")
and [metaobjects](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en "Change the appearance of the page's font size, width, and
color").[[58]](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en "Change the appearance of the page's font size, width, and color")
Many other paradigms are supported via extensions, including [design by contract](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en "
title=")[[59]](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en "
title=")[[60]](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en " title=")
and [logic programming](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en "
title=").[[61]](/w/index.php?title=Special:CreateAccount&returnto=Python+%28programming+language%29 " title=")
Python is often referred to as a *‘[glue language](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en "You're encouraged to log in; however, it's not mandatory.
[o]")’*[[62]](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en "Log in and more options")
because it is purposely designed to be able to integrate components written in other
languages.

Python uses dynamic typing and a combination of [reference counting](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en "User menu") and a cycle-detecting garbage collector for [memory management](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en "User
menu").[[63]](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en " title=")
It uses dynamic [name resolution](/w/index.php?title=Special:CreateAccount&returnto=Python+%28programming+language%29 " title=") ([late binding](/w/index.php?title=Special:UserLogin&returnto=Python+%28programming+language%29 " title=")), which binds method and variable names during program execution.

Python's design offers some support for functional programming in the "[Lisp](/w/index.php?title=Special:UserLogin&returnto=Python+%28programming+language%29 " title=") tradition". It has
`filter`, `map`, and `reduce` functions; [list comprehensions](# "Contents"), [dictionaries](#History "Contents"), [sets](#Design_philosophy_and_features "Contents"), and [generator](#Design_philosophy_and_features "Contents")
expressions.[[64]](#Enhancement_Proposals "Contents") The standard library has two
modules (`itertools` and `functools`) that implement functional tools borrowed from [Haskell](#Indentation "Contents") and [Standard ML](#Indentation
"Contents").[[65]](#Indentation "Contents")

Python's core philosophy is summarized in the [Zen of Python](#Statements_and_control_flow "Contents") (PEP 20) written by [Tim Peters](#Statements_and_control_flow "Contents"), which includes aphorisms such as
these:[[66]](#Expressions "Contents")

- Explicit is better than implicit.
- Simple is better than complex.
- Readability counts.
- Special cases aren't special enough to break the rules.
- Although practicality beats purity, errors should never pass silently, unless explicitly silenced.
- There should be one—and preferably only one—obvious way to do it.

However, Python has received criticism for violating these principles and adding
unnecessary language bloat.[[67]](#Code_examples "Contents") Responses to these criticisms
note that the Zen of Python is a guideline rather than a rule.[[68]](#Development_environments "Contents")
The addition of some new features had been controversial: Guido van Rossum resigned
as *Benevolent Dictator for Life* after conflict about adding the assignment expression
operator in Python 3.8.[[69]](#Limitations_of_the_reference_implementation "Contents")[[70]](#Other_implementations "Contents")

Nevertheless, rather than building all functionality into its core, Python was designed
to be highly [extensible](#Unsupported_implementations "Contents") through modules. This compact modularity has made it particularly popular as a means of adding programmable interfaces to existing applications. Van Rossum's vision of a small core language with a large standard library and an easily extensible interpreter stemmed from his frustrations with ABC, which represented the opposite
approach.[[11]](#Performance "Contents")

Python claims to strive for a simpler, less-cluttered [syntax](#Language_development "Contents") and grammar, while giving developers a choice in their coding methodology. Python lacks [`do .. while`
loops](#Language_development "Contents"), which [Rossum](#Naming "Contents") considered
harmful.[[71]](#Languages_influenced_by_Python "Contents") In contrast to [Perl](#See_also "Contents")'s motto "[there is more than one way to do it](#See_also "Contents")", Python advocates an approach where "there should be one – and preferably only one – obvious way to do
it".[[66]](#References "Contents") In practice, however, Python provides many ways
to achieve a given goal. There are at least three ways to format a string literal,
with no certainty as to which one a programmer should use.[[72]](#Further_reading "Contents")
[Alex Martelli](#Further_reading "Contents") is a [Fellow](#External_links "Contents") at the [Python Software Foundation](#External_links "Contents") and Python book author; he wrote that "To describe something as 'clever' is
*not* considered a compliment in the Python culture."[[73]](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "vector-dropdown vector-page-titlebar-toc vector-button-flush-left")

Python's developers typically prioritize readability over performance. For example,
they reject patches to non-critical parts of the [CPython](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "vector-dropdown-checkbox ") reference implementation that would offer increases in speed that do not justify the cost of clarity and
readability.[[74]](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal)
"vector-unpinned-container")[*[failed verification](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "Python (programmeertaal) –
Afrikaans")*] Execution speed can be improved by moving speed-critical functions
to extension modules written in languages such as [C](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "Python (programmeertaal) – Afrikaans"), or by using a [just-in-time compiler](af "Python (programmeertaal)") like [PyPy](https://als.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(Programmiersprache) "Python (Programmiersprache) – Alemannic"). Also, it is possible to transpile to other languages. However, this approach either fails to achieve the expected speed-up, since Python is a very [dynamic language](https://an.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Aragonese"), or only a restricted subset of Python is compiled (with potential minor semantic
changes).[[75]](https://ar.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D8%A8%D8%A7%D9%8A%D8%AB%D9%88%D9%86_(%D9%84%D8%BA%D8%A9_%D8%A8%D8%B1%D9%85%D8%AC%D8%A9) "good article")

Python is meant to be a fun language to use.[[76]](https://as.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E0%A6%AA%E0%A6%BE%E0%A6%87%E0%A6%A5%E0%A6%A8 "পাইথন –
Assamese"): 3  This goal is reflected in the name – a tribute to the British
comedy group [Monty Python](https://az.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(proqramla%C5%9Fd%C4%B1rma_dili) "Python (proqramlaşdırma dili) –
Azerbaijani")[[77]](https://azb.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D9%BE%D8%A7%DB%8C%D8%AA%D9%88%D9%86 "پایتون – South Azerbaijani")
– and in playful approaches to some tutorials and reference materials. For instance,
some code examples use the terms "spam" and "eggs" (in reference to [a Monty Python sketch](https://ban.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Balinese")), rather than the typical terms ["foo" and "bar"](ban "Python –
Balinese").[[76]](be-tarask "Python")[[78]](be "Python (мова праграмавання) – Belarusian")

A common [neologism](https://bg.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Bulgarian") in the Python community is
*pythonic*, which has a broad range of meanings related to program style: Pythonic
code may use Python [idioms](https://bh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E0%A4%AA%E0%A4%BE%E0%A4%87%E0%A4%A5%E0%A4%A8 "Python") well; be natural or show fluency in the language; or conform with Python's minimalist philosophy and emphasis on
readability.[[79]](https://bn.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E0%A6%AA%E0%A6%BE%E0%A6%87%E0%A6%A5%E0%A6%A8_(%E0%A6%AA%E0%A7%8D%E0%A6%B0%E0%A7%8B%E0%A6%97%E0%A7%8D%E0%A6%B0%E0%A6%BE%E0%A6%AE%E0%A6%BF%E0%A6%82_%E0%A6%AD%E0%A6%BE%E0%A6%B7%E0%A6%BE) "পাইথন (প্রোগ্রামিং ভাষা) – Bangla")


### Enhancement Proposals

**Python Enhancement Proposals**[[note 1]](https://bug.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Buginese")
are a design document for either providing information to the [Python](https://ca.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Catalan") community, or proposal for new feature in
Python.[[80]](https://ckb.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D9%BE%D8%A7%DB%8C%D8%AA%DB%86%D9%86_(%D8%B2%D9%85%D8%A7%D9%86%DB%8C_%D8%A8%DB%95%D8%B1%D9%86%D8%A7%D9%85%DB%95%D8%B3%D8%A7%D8%B2%DB%8C) "پایتۆن (زمانی بەرنامەسازی) – Central Kurdish")
PEPs are intented to explain new processes in Python, provide [naming conventions](ckb "پایتۆن (زمانی بەرنامەسازی) – Central Kurdish") or document the processes in the
language.[[81]](da "Python (programmeringssprog) – Danish") PEPs are overseen
by Python Steering Council.[[81]](dtp "Python (boros tokud)")

There are 3 kinds of PEPs, with those are being *standards track PEP*[[note 2]](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python –
Spanish"), *Informational PEP*[[note 3]](https://fa.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D9%BE%D8%A7%DB%8C%D8%AA%D9%88%D9%86_(%D8%B2%D8%A8%D8%A7%D9%86_%D8%A8%D8%B1%D9%86%D8%A7%D9%85%D9%87%E2%80%8C%D9%86%D9%88%DB%8C%D8%B3%DB%8C) "پایتون (زبان برنامه‌نویسی) – Persian")
and *Process PEP*s[[note 4]](https://fj.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Fijian")
which has their own unique meanings.[[80]](https://gl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python –
Galician")[[82]](gu "પાયથોન (પ્રોગ્રામિંગ ભાષા) – Gujarati")
They were firstly introduced in 2000, inspired by other RfCs (requests for comments)
and Design Enhancement Proposals.[[82]](he "פייתון – Hebrew") Most known
PEPs are PEP – 1, PEP – 8, [PEP – 20](https://hu.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programoz%C3%A1si_nyelv) "Python (programozási nyelv) – Hungarian"),
PEP – 257 and others.[[82]](https://io.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Ido")


## Syntax and semantics

Main article: [Python syntax and semantics](ja "Python")
Python is meant to be an easily readable language. Its formatting is visually uncluttered
and often uses English keywords where other languages use punctuation. Unlike many
other languages, it does not use [curly brackets](https://ka.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E1%83%9E%E1%83%90%E1%83%98%E1%83%97%E1%83%9D%E1%83%9C%E1%83%98_(%E1%83%9E%E1%83%A0%E1%83%9D%E1%83%92%E1%83%A0%E1%83%90%E1%83%9B%E1%83%98%E1%83%A0%E1%83%94%E1%83%91%E1%83%98%E1%83%A1_%E1%83%94%E1%83%9C%E1%83%90) "პაითონი (პროგრამირების ენა) – Georgian") to delimit blocks, and semicolons after statements are allowed but rarely used. It has fewer syntactic exceptions and special cases than [C](ka "პაითონი (პროგრამირების ენა) – Georgian") or [Pascal](ka "პაითონი (პროგრამირების
ენა)").[[83]](https://kaa.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmalast%C4%B1r%C4%B1w_tili) "Python (programmalastırıw tili) – Kara-Kalpak")


### Indentation

Further information: [Python syntax and semantics § Indentation](https://ko.wikipedia.org/wiki/%ED%8C%8C%EC%9D%B4%EC%8D%AC "파이썬 – Korean")
Python uses [whitespace](https://ks.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D9%BE%D8%A7%DB%8C%D8%AA%DA%BE%D9%8E%D9%86_(%D9%BE%D8%B1%D9%9B%D9%88%DA%AF%D8%B1%D8%A7%D9%85%D9%90%D9%86%D9%9B%DA%AF_%D8%B2%D9%8E%D8%A8%D8%A7%D9%86) "پایتھَن (پرٛوگرامِنٛگ زَبان) – Kashmiri") indentation, rather than curly brackets or keywords, to delimit [blocks](ks "پایتھَن (پرٛوگرامِنٛگ زَبان) – Kashmiri"). An increase in indentation comes after certain statements; a decrease in indentation signifies the end of the current
block.[[84]](ku "Python (zimanê bernamesaziyê) – Kurdish") Thus, the program's
visual structure accurately represents its semantic structure.[[85]](https://la.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(lingua_programmationis) "Python (lingua programmationis) – Latin")
This feature is sometimes termed the [off-side rule](https://lmo.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python (lingua programmationis)"). Some other languages use indentation this way; but in most, indentation has no semantic meaning. The recommended indent size is four
spaces.[[86]](lo "ໄພທອນ (ພາສາໂປຣແກຣມ) – Lao")


### Statements and control flow

Python's [statements](https://lt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Lithuanian") include the following:

- The [assignment](https://lv.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programm%C4%93%C5%A1anas_valoda) "Python (programmēšanas valoda) – Latvian") statement, using a single equals sign `=`
- The `[if](lv "Python (programmēšanas valoda) – Latvian")` statement, which conditionally executes a block of code, along with `[else](https://mk.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%9F%D0%B0%D1%98%D1%82%D0%BE%D0%BD_(%D0%BF%D1%80%D0%BE%D0%B3%D1%80%D0%B0%D0%BC%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%B8_%D1%98%D0%B0%D0%B7%D0%B8%D0%BA) "Пајтон (програмски јазик) – Macedonian")` and `elif` (a contraction of `[else if](mk "Пајтон (програмски јазик) – Macedonian")`)
- The `[for](https://ml.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E0%B4%AA%E0%B5%88%E0%B4%A4%E0%B5%8D%E0%B4%A4%E0%B5%BA_(%E0%B4%AA%E0%B5%8D%E0%B4%B0%E0%B5%8B%E0%B4%97%E0%B5%8D%E0%B4%B0%E0%B4%BE%E0%B4%AE%E0%B4%BF%E0%B4%99%E0%B5%8D%E0%B4%99%E0%B5%8D_%E0%B4%AD%E0%B4%BE%E0%B4%B7) "പൈത്തൺ (പ്രോഗ്രാമിങ്ങ് ഭാഷ) – Malayalam")` statement, which iterates over an *iterable* object, capturing each element to a variable for use by the attached block; the variable is not deleted when the loop finishes
- The `[while](ml "പൈത്തൺ (പ്രോഗ്രാമിങ്ങ് ഭാഷ) – Malayalam")` statement, which executes a block of code as long as boolean condition is true
- The `[try](https://mn.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Mongolian")` statement, which allows exceptions raised in its attached code block to be caught and handled by `except` clauses (or new syntax `except*` in Python 3.11 for exception groups);[[87]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "client-js vector-feature-language-in-header-enabled vector-feature-language-in-main-menu-disabled vector-feature-language-in-main-page-header-disabled vector-feature-page-tools-pinned-disabled vector-feature-toc-pinned-clientpref-1 vector-feature-main-menu-pinned-disabled vector-feature-limited-width-clientpref-1 vector-feature-limited-width-content-enabled vector-feature-custom-font-size-clientpref-1 vector-feature-appearance-pinned-clientpref-1 skin-theme-clientpref-day vector-sticky-header-enabled vector-toc-available skin-thumbsize-clientpref-standard") the `try` statement also ensures that clean-up code in a `finally` block is always run regardless of how the block exits
- The `raise` statement, used to raise a specified exception or re-raise a caught exception
- The `class` statement, which executes a block of code and attaches its local namespace to a [class](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ext.globalCssJs.user.styles"), for use in object-oriented programming
- The `def` statement, which defines a [function](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ext.globalCssJs.user.styles") or [method](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ext.globalCssJs.user.styles")
- The `[with](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ext.globalCssJs.user.styles")` statement, which encloses a code block within a context manager, allowing [resource-acquisition-is-initialization](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "ext.globalCssJs.user.styles") (RAII)-like behavior and replacing a common try/finally idiom[[88]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") Examples of a context include acquiring a [lock](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") before some code is run, and then releasing the lock; or opening and then closing a [file](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia")
- The `[break](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia")` statement, which exits a loop
- The `continue` statement, which skips the rest of the current iteration and continues with the next
- The `del` statement, which removes a variable—deleting the reference from the name to the value, and producing an error if the variable is referred to before it is redefined[[d]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia")
- The `pass` statement, serving as a [NOP](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") (i.e., no operation), which is syntactically needed to create an empty code block
- The `[assert](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia")` statement, used in debugging to check for conditions that should apply
- The `yield` statement, which returns a value from a [generator](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") function (and also an operator); used to implement [coroutines](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia")
- The `return` statement, used to return a value from a function
- The `[import](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia")` and `from` statements, used to import modules whose functions or variables can be used in the current program. Python 3.15 adds a new functionality to lazily import with a new keyword: "The `lazy` keyword works with both `import` and `from ... import` statements."[[41]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia")
- The `match` and `case` statements, analogous to a [switch statement](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") construct, which compares an expression against one or more cases as a control-flow measure

The assignment statement (`=`) binds a name as a [reference](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") to a separate, dynamically allocated [object](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia"). Variables may subsequently be rebound at any time to any object. In Python, a variable name is a generic reference holder without a fixed [data type](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia"); however, it always refers to
*some* object with a type. This is called [dynamic typing](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia")—in contrast to [statically-typed](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") languages, where each variable may contain only a value of a certain type.

Python does not support [tail call](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") optimization or [first-class continuations](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia"); according to Van Rossum, the language never
will.[[89]](//upload.wikimedia.org "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia")[[90]](//upload.wikimedia.org "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia")
However, better support for [coroutine](//upload.wikimedia.org "Wikipedia (en)")-like functionality is provided by extending Python's
generators.[[91]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programming_language) "Wikipedia Atom feed")
Before 2.5, generators were [lazy](/w/index.php?title=Special:RecentChanges&feed=atom "Wikipedia Atom feed") [iterators](//meta.wikimedia.org "></script></head>
<body class="); data was passed unidirectionally out of the generator. From Python 2.5
on, it is possible to pass data back into a generator function; and from version
3.3, data can be passed through multiple stack levels.[[92]](#bodyContent "></script></head>
<body class=")


### Expressions

Python's [expressions](#bodyContent "></script></head>
<body class=") include the following:

- The `+`, `-`, and `*` operators for mathematical addition, subtraction, and multiplication are similar to other languages, but the behavior of division differs. There are two types of division in Python: [floor division](#bodyContent "Main menu") (or integer division) `//`, and floating-point division `/`.[[93]](#bodyContent "Main menu") Python uses the `**` operator for exponentiation.
- Python uses the `+` operator for string concatenation. The language uses the `*` operator for duplicating a string a specified number of times.
- The `@` infix operator is intended to be used by libraries such as [NumPy](/wiki/Main_Page "Main menu") for [matrix multiplication](/wiki/Main_Page "Visit the main page [z]").[[94]](/wiki/Main_Page "Visit the main page [z]")[[95]](/wiki/Main_Page "Visit the main page [z]")
- The syntax `:=`, called the "walrus operator", was introduced in Python 3.8. This operator assigns values to variables as part of a larger expression.[[96]](/wiki/Help:Contents "Guidance on how to use and edit Wikipedia")
- In Python, `==` compares two objects by value. Python's `is` operator may be used to compare object identities (i.e., comparison by reference), and comparisons may be chained—for example, `a <= b <= c`.
- Python uses `and`, `or`, and `not` as Boolean operators.
- Python has a type of expression called a *[list comprehension](/wiki/Special:Search "Search Wikipedia [f]")*, and a more general expression called a *generator expression*.[[64]](/wiki/Special:Search "Search Wikipedia [f]")
- [Anonymous functions](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en "Search Wikipedia [f]") are implemented using [lambda expressions](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en "Search Wikipedia [f]"); however, there may be only one expression in each body.
- Conditional expressions are written as `x if c else y`.[[97]](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en "Change the appearance of the page's font size, width, and color") (This is different in operand order from the `[c ? x : y](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en " title=")` operator common to many other languages.)
- Python makes a distinction between [lists](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en " title=") and [tuples](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en " title="). Lists are written as `[1, 2, 3]`, are mutable, and cannot be used as the keys of dictionaries (since dictionary keys must be [immutable](/w/index.php?title=Special:UserLogin&returnto=Python+%28programming+language%29 "You are encouraged to create an account and log in; however, it is not mandatory") in Python). Tuples, written as `(1, 2, 3)`, are immutable and thus can be used as the keys of dictionaries, provided that all of the tuple's elements are immutable. The `+` operator can be used to concatenate two tuples, which does not directly modify their contents, but produces a new tuple containing the elements of both. For example, given the variable `t` initially equal to `(1, 2, 3)`, executing `t = t + (4, 5)` first evaluates `t + (4, 5)`, which yields `(1, 2, 3, 4, 5)`; this result is then assigned back to `t`—thereby effectively "modifying the contents" of `t` while conforming to the immutable nature of tuple objects. Parentheses are optional for tuples in unambiguous contexts.[[98]](#Indentation "Contents")
- Python features *sequence unpacking* where multiple expressions, each evaluating to something assignable (e.g., a variable or a writable property) are associated just as in forming tuple literal; as a whole, the results are then put on the left-hand side of the equal sign in an assignment statement. This statement expects an *iterable* object on the right-hand side of the equal sign to produce the same number of values as the writable expressions on the left-hand side; while iterating, the statement assigns each of the values produced on the right to the corresponding expression on the left.[[99]](#Expressions "Contents")
- Python has a "string format" operator `%` that functions analogously to `[printf](#Typing "Contents")` format strings in the C language—e.g. `"spam=%s eggs=%d" % ("blah", 2)` evaluates to `"spam=blah eggs=2"`. In Python 2.6+ and 3+, this operator was supplemented by the `format()` method of the `str` class, e.g., `"spam={0} eggs={1}".format("blah", 2)`. Python 3.6 added "f-strings": `spam = "blah"; eggs = 2; f'spam={spam} eggs={eggs}'`.[[100]](#Performance "Contents")
- Strings in Python can be [concatenated](#Language_development "Contents") by "adding" them (using the same operator as for adding integers and floats); e.g., `"spam" + "eggs"` returns `"spameggs"`. If strings contain numbers, they are concatenated as strings rather than as integers, e.g. `"2" + "2"` returns `"22"`.
- Python supports [string literals](#Sources "Contents") in several ways:- Delimited by single or double quotation marks; single and double quotation marks have equivalent functionality (unlike in [Unix shells](#Further_reading "Contents"), [Perl](#Further_reading "Contents"), and Perl-influenced languages). Both marks use the backslash (`\`) as an [escape character](#Further_reading "Contents"). [String interpolation](#External_links "Contents") became available in Python 3.6 as "formatted string literals".[[100]](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "Contents")
- Triple-quoted, i.e., starting and ending with three single or double quotation marks; this may span multiple lines and function like [here documents](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "Table of Contents") in shells, Perl, and [Ruby](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "vector-dropdown-checkbox ").
- [Raw string](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "vector-dropdown-label cdx-button cdx-button--fake-button cdx-button--fake-button--enabled cdx-button--weight-quiet cdx-button--icon-only ") varieties, denoted by prefixing the string literal with `r`. Escape sequences are not interpreted; hence raw strings are useful where literal backslashes are common, such as in [regular expressions](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "vector-unpinned-container") and [Windows](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "p-lang-btn")-style paths. (Compare "`@`-quoting" in [C#](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "p-lang-btn").)
- Python has [array index](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "Python (programmeertaal) – Afrikaans") and [array slicing](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "Python (programmeertaal) – Afrikaans") expressions in lists, which are written as
`a[key]`, `a[start:stop]` or `a[start:stop:step]`. Indexes are [zero-based](https://as.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E0%A6%AA%E0%A6%BE%E0%A6%87%E0%A6%A5%E0%A6%A8 "পাইথন – Assamese"), and negative indexes are relative to the end. Slices take elements from the
*start* index up to, but not including, the *stop* index. The (optional) third slice
[parameter](https://ast.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Asturian"), called *step*
or *stride*, allows elements to be skipped or reversed. Slice indexes may be omitted—for
example, `a[:]` returns a copy of the entire list. Each element of a slice is a [shallow copy](https://ban.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Balinese").

In Python, a distinction between expressions and statements is rigidly enforced,
in contrast to languages such as [Common Lisp](https://be-tarask.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Belarusian (Taraškievica orthography)"), [Scheme](https://be-tarask.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Belarusian (Taraškievica orthography)"), or [Ruby](be-tarask "Python"). This distinction leads to duplicating some functionality, for example:

- [List comprehensions](https://be.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(%D0%BC%D0%BE%D0%B2%D0%B0_%D0%BF%D1%80%D0%B0%D0%B3%D1%80%D0%B0%D0%BC%D0%B0%D0%B2%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BD%D1%8F) "Python (мова праграмавання) – Belarusian") vs. `for`-loops
- [Conditional](be "Python (мова праграмавання) – Belarusian") expressions vs. `if` blocks
- The `eval()` vs. `exec()` built-in functions (in Python 2, `exec` is a statement); the former function is for expressions, while the latter is for statements

A statement cannot be part of an expression; because of this restriction, expressions
such as list and `dict` comprehensions (and lambda expressions) cannot contain statements.
As a particular case, an assignment statement such as `a = 1` cannot be part of the conditional
expression of a conditional statement.


### Typing
[![Page semi-protected](//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/1/1b/Semi-protection-shackle.svg/20px-Semi-protection-shackle.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail "Python (programski jezik) – Bosnian")](https://bs.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programski_jezik) "Python (lavar programmiñ)")
The standard type hierarchy in Python 3
Python uses [duck typing](https://ceb.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programming_language) "Python (programming language) – Cebuano"), and it has typed objects but untyped variable names. Type constraints are not checked at definition time; rather, operations on an object may fail at usage time, indicating that the object is not of an appropriate type. Despite being [dynamically typed](https://ckb.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D9%BE%D8%A7%DB%8C%D8%AA%DB%86%D9%86_(%D8%B2%D9%85%D8%A7%D9%86%DB%8C_%D8%A8%DB%95%D8%B1%D9%86%D8%A7%D9%85%DB%95%D8%B3%D8%A7%D8%B2%DB%8C) "پایتۆن (زمانی بەرنامەسازی) – Central Kurdish"), Python is [strongly typed](https://ckb.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D9%BE%D8%A7%DB%8C%D8%AA%DB%86%D9%86_(%D8%B2%D9%85%D8%A7%D9%86%DB%8C_%D8%A8%DB%95%D8%B1%D9%86%D8%A7%D9%85%DB%95%D8%B3%D8%A7%D8%B2%DB%8C) "پایتۆن (زمانی بەرنامەسازی) – Central Kurdish"), forbidding operations that are poorly defined (e.g., adding a number and a string) rather than quietly attempting to interpret them.

Python allows programmers to define their own types using [classes](https://cs.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Czech"), most often for [object-oriented programming](https://cs.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Czech"). New [instances](https://cy.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(iaith_raglennu) "Python") of classes are constructed by calling the class, for example, `SpamClass()`
or `EggsClass()`); the classes are instances of the [metaclass](https://eo.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programlingvo) "Python (programlingvo) – Esperanto") `type`
(which is an instance of itself), thereby allowing metaprogramming and [reflection](eo "Python (programlingvo) – Esperanto").

Before version 3.0, Python had two kinds of classes, both using the same syntax:
*old-style* and *new-style*.[[101]](https://et.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeerimiskeel) "Python (programmeerimiskeel) – Estonian")
Current Python versions support the semantics of only the new style.

Python supports [optional type annotations](https://eu.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(informatika) "Python (informatika) –
Basque").[[5]](https://fa.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D9%BE%D8%A7%DB%8C%D8%AA%D9%88%D9%86_(%D8%B2%D8%A8%D8%A7%D9%86_%D8%A8%D8%B1%D9%86%D8%A7%D9%85%D9%87%E2%80%8C%D9%86%D9%88%DB%8C%D8%B3%DB%8C) "پایتون (زبان برنامه‌نویسی) –
Persian")[[102]](https://fi.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(ohjelmointikieli) "Python (ohjelmointikieli) – Finnish")
These annotations are not enforced by the language, but may be used by external tools
such as **mypy** to catch errors. Python includes a module `typing` including several type
names for type annotations.[[103]](https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(langage) "Python (langage) –
French")[[104]](https://gl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Galician") Also,
mypy supports a Python compiler called mypyc, which leverages type annotations for
optimization.[[105]](glk "پايتؤن (برنامه‌نيويسي زوان)")

Python's built-in type `memoryview` does though support both 64-bit doubles and 32-bit floats, and Python's standard library `struct` module additionally supports 16-bit half-floats. Python packages like NumPy and Pandas may also support 32-bit floats or more, e.g. `numpy.half`; though support for half-floats is often incomplete or non-existent in most packages.[[108]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") 16-bit [bfloat](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") has support in a few packages.[[109]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") |

### Arithmetic operations

Python includes conventional symbols for arithmetic operators (`+`, `-`, `*`, `/`), the
floor-division operator `//`, and the [modulo operator](#Indentation "Contents") `%`.
(With the modulo operator, a remainder can be negative, e.g., `4 % -3 == -2`.) Python also offers
the `**` symbol for [exponentiation](#Expressions "Contents"), e.g. `5**3 == 125` and `9**0.5 == 3.0`, as well
as the matrix‑multiplication operator `@`.[[112]](#Typing "Contents") These operators
work as in traditional mathematics; with the same [precedence rules](#Arithmetic_operations "Contents"), the [infix](#Function_syntax "Contents") operators `+`
and `-` can also be [unary](#Function_syntax "Contents"), to represent positive and negative numbers respectively.

Division between integers produces floating-point results. The behavior of division
has changed significantly over time:[[113]](#Libraries "Contents")

- The current version of Python (i.e., since 3.0) changed the `/` operator to always represent floating-point division, e.g., `5/2 == 2.5`.
- The floor division `//` operator was introduced, meaning that `7//3 == 2`, `-7//3 == -3`, `7.5//3 == 2.0`, and `-7.5//3 == -3.0`. For Python 2.7, adding the `from __future__ import division` statement allows a module in Python 2.7 to use Python 3.x rules for division (see above).

In Python terms, the `/` operator represents *true division* (or simply *division*),
while the `//` operator represents *floor division.* Before version 3.0, the `/` operator
represents *classic division*.[[113]](#Performance "Contents")

[Rounding](#Performance "Contents") towards negative infinity, though a different method than in most languages, adds consistency to Python. For instance, this rounding implies that the equation `(a + b)//b == a//b + 1`
is always true. Also, the rounding implies that the equation `b*(a//b) + a%b == a` is valid for both
positive and negative values of `a`. As expected, the result of `a%b` lies in the [half-open interval](#External_links "Contents") [0,
*b*), where `b` is a positive integer; however, maintaining the validity of the equation
requires that the result must lie in the interval (*b*, 0] when `b` is negative.[[114]](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "Contents")

Python provides a `round` function for rounding a float to the nearest integer. For [tie-breaking](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "button"),
Python 3 uses the *round to even* method: `round(1.5)` and `round(2.5)` both produce `2`.[[115]](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "vector-unpinned-container")
Python versions before 3 used the [round-away-from-zero](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "p-lang-btn") method: `round(0.5)`
is `1.0`, and `round(-0.5)` is `−1.0`.[[116]](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "Python (programmeertaal) – Afrikaans")

Python allows Boolean expressions that contain multiple equality relations to be
consistent with general usage in mathematics. For example, the expression `a < b < c` tests
whether `a` is less than `b` and `b` is less than `c`.[[117]](af "Python (programmeertaal)")
C-derived languages interpret this expression differently: in C, the expression would
first evaluate `a < b`, resulting in 0 or 1, and that result would then be compared with
`c`.[[118]](https://an.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Aragonese")

Python uses [arbitrary-precision arithmetic](https://ar.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D8%A8%D8%A7%D9%8A%D8%AB%D9%88%D9%86_(%D9%84%D8%BA%D8%A9_%D8%A8%D8%B1%D9%85%D8%AC%D8%A9) "good article") for all integer operations. The `Decimal`
type/class in the `decimal` module provides [decimal floating-point numbers](ar "بايثون (لغة برمجة) – Arabic") to a pre-defined arbitrary precision with several rounding
modes.[[119]](as "পাইথন") The `Fraction` class in the `fractions` module provides arbitrary
precision for [rational numbers](ast "Python").[[120]](az "Python (proqramlaşdırma dili)")

Due to Python's extensive mathematics library and the third-party library [NumPy](https://azb.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D9%BE%D8%A7%DB%8C%D8%AA%D9%88%D9%86 "پایتون – South Azerbaijani"), the language is frequently used for scientific scripting in tasks such as numerical data processing and
manipulation.[[121]](ban "Python")[[122]](https://be.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(%D0%BC%D0%BE%D0%B2%D0%B0_%D0%BF%D1%80%D0%B0%D0%B3%D1%80%D0%B0%D0%BC%D0%B0%D0%B2%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BD%D1%8F) "Python (мова праграмавання) – Belarusian")


### Function syntax

[Functions](be "Python (мова праграмавання) – Belarusian") are created in Python by using the `def`
keyword. A function is defined similarly to how it is called, by first providing
the function name and then the required parameters. Here is an example of a function
that prints its inputs:

```def printer(input1, input2 = "already there"):    print(input1)    print(input2)printer("hello")# 
Example output:# hello# already there```

To assign a default value to a function parameter in case no actual value is provided 
at run time, variable-definition syntax can be used inside the function header.


## Code examples

["Hello, World!" program](https://bug.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Buginese"):

```print('Hello, World!')```

Program to calculate the [factorial](ceb "Python (programming language) – Cebuano") of a non-negative integer:

```text = input('Type a number, and its factorial will be printed: ')n = int(text)if 
n < 0:    raise ValueError('You must enter a non-negative integer')factorial = 
1for i in range(2, n + 1):    factorial \*= iprint(factorial)```


## Libraries

Python's large standard library[[123]](https://et.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeerimiskeel) "Python (programmeerimiskeel) – Estonian") 
is commonly cited as one of its greatest strengths. For Internet-facing applications, 
many standard formats and protocols such as [MIME](https://eu.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(informatika) "Python (informatika) – Basque") and [HTTP](https://eu.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(informatika) "Python (informatika) – Basque") are supported. The language includes modules for creating [graphical user interfaces](eu "Python (informatika)"), connecting to [relational databases](https://fa.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D9%BE%D8%A7%DB%8C%D8%AA%D9%88%D9%86_(%D8%B2%D8%A8%D8%A7%D9%86_%D8%A8%D8%B1%D9%86%D8%A7%D9%85%D9%87%E2%80%8C%D9%86%D9%88%DB%8C%D8%B3%DB%8C) "پایتون (زبان برنامه‌نویسی) – Persian"), [generating pseudorandom numbers](fa "پایتون (زبان برنامه‌نویسی) – Persian"), arithmetic with arbitrary-precision
decimals,[[119]](https://fi.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(ohjelmointikieli) "Python (ohjelmointikieli) – Finnish") 
manipulating [regular expressions](https://fj.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Fijian"), and [unit testing](https://fj.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Fijian").

Some parts of the standard library are covered by specifications—for example, the 
[Web Server Gateway Interface](https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(langage) "Python (langage) – French") (WSGI) implementation `wsgiref` 
follows PEP 333[[124]](gl "Python – Galician")—but most parts are specified by 
their code, internal documentation, and [test suites](https://glk.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D9%BE%D8%A7%D9%8A%D8%AA%D8%A4%D9%86_(%D8%A8%D8%B1%D9%86%D8%A7%D9%85%D9%87%E2%80%8C%D9%86%D9%8A%D9%88%D9%8A%D8%B3%D9%8A_%D8%B2%D9%88%D8%A7%D9%86) "پايتؤن (برنامه‌نيويسي زوان) – Gilaki"). However, because most of the standard library is cross-platform Python code, only a few modules must be altered or rewritten for variant implementations.

As of 13 March 2025,[[update]](https://ha.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_programming_language "Python programming language – Hausa")[Python Package Index](https://hi.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E0%A4%AA%E0%A4%BE%E0%A4%87%E0%A4%A5%E0%A4%A8 "पाइथन – Hindi") (PyPI), the official repository for third-party Python software, contains over
614,339[[125]](https://hr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programski_jezik) "Python (programski jezik) – Croatian") 
packages.


## Development environments

See also: [Comparison of integrated development environments § Python](ia "Python (linguage de programmation)")
Most[*[which?](https://is.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(forritunarm%C3%A1l) "Python (forritunarmál) –
Icelandic")*] Python implementations (including CPython) include a [read–eval–print loop](https://it.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Italian") (REPL); this permits the environment to function as a [command line interpreter](https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Japanese"), with which users enter statements sequentially and receive results
immediately.[[126]](https://ka.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E1%83%9E%E1%83%90%E1%83%98%E1%83%97%E1%83%9D%E1%83%9C%E1%83%98_(%E1%83%9E%E1%83%A0%E1%83%9D%E1%83%92%E1%83%A0%E1%83%90%E1%83%9B%E1%83%98%E1%83%A0%E1%83%94%E1%83%91%E1%83%98%E1%83%A1_%E1%83%94%E1%83%9C%E1%83%90) "paiton")

Also, CPython is bundled with an [integrated development environment (IDE)](ka "პაითონი (პროგრამირების ენა) – Georgian") called [IDLE](ka "პაითონი (პროგრამირების ენა) –
Georgian"),[[127]](https://kaa.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmalast%C4%B1r%C4%B1w_tili) "Python (programmalastırıw tili) – Kara-Kalpak") 
which is oriented toward beginners.[*[citation needed](km "ផាយថុន – Khmer")*][*[by 
whom?](ks "پایتھَن (پرٛوگرامِنٛگ زَبان) – Kashmiri")*]

Other shells, including [IDLE](https://ku.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(ziman%C3%AA_bernamesaziy%C3%AA) "Python (zimanê bernamesaziyê) – Kurdish") and [IPython](ku "Python (zimanê bernamesaziyê)"), add additional capabilities such as improved auto-completion, session-state retention, and [syntax highlighting](https://ky.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python –
Kyrgyz").[[127]](https://la.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(lingua_programmationis) "Python (lingua programmationis) –
Latin")[[128]](lmo "Python – Lombard")

Standard desktop IDEs include [PyCharm](https://lo.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E0%BB%84%E0%BA%9E%E0%BA%97%E0%BA%AD%E0%BA%99_(%E0%BA%9E%E0%BA%B2%E0%BA%AA%E0%BA%B2%E0%BB%82%E0%BA%9B%E0%BA%A3%E0%BB%81%E0%BA%81%E0%BA%A3%E0%BA%A1) "ໄພທອນ (ພາສາໂປຣແກຣມ) – Lao"), [Spyder](lo "ໄພທອນ (ພາສາໂປຣແກຣມ) – Lao"), and [Visual Studio Code](lo "ໄພທອນ (ພາສາໂປຣແກຣມ) –
Lao");[[129]](lt "Python – Lithuanian") there are [web browser](https://lv.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programm%C4%93%C5%A1anas_valoda) "Python (programmēšanas valoda) – Latvian")-based IDEs, such as the following environments:

- [Jupyter Notebooks](lv "Python (programmēšanas valoda)"), an open-source interactive computing platform;[[130]](mk "Пајтон (програмски јазик) – Macedonian")
- [PythonAnywhere](https://ml.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E0%B4%AA%E0%B5%88%E0%B4%A4%E0%B5%8D%E0%B4%A4%E0%B5%BA_(%E0%B4%AA%E0%B5%8D%E0%B4%B0%E0%B5%8B%E0%B4%97%E0%B5%8D%E0%B4%B0%E0%B4%BE%E0%B4%AE%E0%B4%BF%E0%B4%99%E0%B5%8D%E0%B4%99%E0%B5%8D_%E0%B4%AD%E0%B4%BE%E0%B4%B7) "പൈത്തൺ (പ്രോഗ്രാമിങ്ങ് ഭാഷ) – Malayalam"), a browser-based IDE and hosting environment; and
- Canopy, a commercial IDE from [Enthought](ml "പൈത്തൺ (പ്രോഗ്രാമിങ്ങ് ഭാഷ) – Malayalam") that emphasizes [scientific computing](ml "പൈത്തൺ (പ്രോഗ്രാമിങ്ങ് ഭാഷ) – Malayalam").[[131]](https://mn.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Mongolian")[[132]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "client-js vector-feature-language-in-header-enabled vector-feature-language-in-main-menu-disabled vector-feature-language-in-main-page-header-disabled vector-feature-page-tools-pinned-disabled vector-feature-toc-pinned-clientpref-1 vector-feature-main-menu-pinned-disabled vector-feature-limited-width-clientpref-1 vector-feature-limited-width-content-enabled vector-feature-custom-font-size-clientpref-1 vector-feature-appearance-pinned-clientpref-1 skin-theme-clientpref-day vector-sticky-header-enabled vector-toc-available skin-thumbsize-clientpref-standard")

standard[[133]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") 
since version 3.11. Older versions use the [C89](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") standard with several select [C99](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") features, but third-party extensions are not limited to older C versions—e.g., they can be implemented using C11 or
C++.[[134]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) -
Wikipedia")[[135]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") 
CPython [compiles](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") Python programs into an intermediate [bytecode](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) -
