which is then executed by a [virtual machine](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) -
Wikipedia").[[137]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") 
CPython is distributed with a large standard library written in a combination of 
C and native Python.

CPython is available for many platforms, including Windows and most modern [Unix-like](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") systems, including macOS (and [Apple M1](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") Macs, since
Python 3.9.1, using an experimental installer). Starting with Python 3.9, 
the Python installer intentionally fails to install on [Windows 7](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") and
8;[[138]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) -
Wikipedia")[[139]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") 
[Windows XP](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") was supported until
Python 3.5. Old Python versions unofficially support [VMS](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") (mostly
supporting[[140]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) -
Wikipedia")) and [OpenVMS](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") x86-64 has Python 3.10
support.[[141]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=site.styles&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 "Python (programming language) -
Wikipedia")[[142]](//upload.wikimedia.org "Python (programming language) - Wikipedia") 
Platform portability was one of Python's earliest priorities.[[143]](/static/apple-touch/wikipedia.png "Wikipedia (en)") 
During development of Python 1 and 2, even [OS/2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programming_language) "Wikipedia Atom feed") and [Solaris](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/deed.en "Wikipedia Atom feed") were
supported;[[8]](#bodyContent "></script></head>
<body class=") since that time, support has been dropped for many platforms.

All current Python versions (since 3.7) support only operating systems that feature 
multithreading, by now supporting not nearly as many operating systems (dropping 
many outdated) than in the past.


### Limitations of the reference implementation

- The energy usage of Python with CPython for typically written code is much worse than C by a factor of 75.88.[[144]](#bodyContent "Main menu")
- The throughput of Python with CPython for typically written code is worse than C by a factor of 71.9.[[144]](/wiki/Main_Page "Main menu")
- The average memory usage of CPython for typically written code is worse than C by a factor of 2.4.[[144]](/wiki/Main_Page "Visit the main page [z]")


### Other implementations

All alternative implementations have at least slightly different semantics. For example, 
an alternative may include unordered dictionaries, in contrast to other current Python 
versions. As another example in the larger Python ecosystem, PyPy does not support 
the full C Python API.

Creating an executable with Python often is done by bundling an entire Python interpreter 
into the executable, which causes binary sizes to be massive for small programs,[[145]](/wiki/Main_Page "Visit the main page [z]") 
yet there exist implementations that are capable of truly compiling Python. Alternative 
implementations include the following:

- [PyPy](/wiki/Wikipedia:Contents "Visit the main page [z]") is a faster, compliant interpreter of Python 2.7 and  3.11.[[146]](/wiki/Wikipedia:About "Learn about Wikipedia and how it works")[[147]](/wiki/Help:Contents "Guidance on how to use and edit Wikipedia") PyPy's [just-in-time compiler](/wiki/Help:Contents "Guidance on how to use and edit Wikipedia") often improves speed significantly relative to CPython, but PyPy does not support some libraries written in C.[[148]](/wiki/Wikipedia:File_upload_wizard "A list of recent changes to Wikipedia [r]") PyPy offers support for the [RISC-V](/wiki/Special:SpecialPages "A list of all special pages [q]") instruction-set architecture.
- Codon is an implementation with an [ahead-of-time (AOT) compiler](/wiki/Main_Page "Search Wikipedia [f]"), which compiles a statically-typed Python-like language whose "syntax and semantics are nearly identical to Python's, there are some notable differences"[[149]](/wiki/Special:Search "Search Wikipedia [f]") For example, Codon uses 64-bit machine integers for speed, not arbitrarily as with Python; Codon developers claim that speedups over CPython are usually on the order of ten to a hundred times. Codon compiles to machine code (via [LLVM](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en "Search Wikipedia [f]")) and supports native multithreading.[[150]](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en "Search Wikipedia [f]")  Codon can also compile to Python extension modules that can be imported and used from Python.
- [MicroPython](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en "Search Wikipedia [f]") and [CircuitPython](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en "Search Wikipedia [f]") are Python 3 variants that are optimized for [microcontrollers](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en "Change the appearance of the page's font size, width, and color"), including the [Lego Mindstorms EV3](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en "Change the appearance of the page's font size, width, and color").[[151]](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en "Change the appearance of the page's font size, width, and color")
- Pyston is a variant of the Python runtime that uses just-in-time compilation to speed up execution of Python programs.[[152]](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en " title=")
- Cinder is a performance-oriented fork of CPython 3.8 that features a number of optimizations, including bytecode inline caching, eager evaluation of coroutines, a method-at-a-time [JIT](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en " title="), and an experimental bytecode compiler.[[153]](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en " title=")
- The Snek[[154]](/w/index.php?title=Special:UserLogin&returnto=Python+%28programming+language%29 " title=")[[155]](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en "Log in and more options")[[156]](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en "User menu") embedded computing language "is Python-inspired, but it is not Python. It is possible to write Snek programs that run under a full Python system, but most Python programs will not run under Snek."[[157]](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en " title=") Snek is compatible with 8-bit [AVR microcontrollers](/w/index.php?title=Special:CreateAccount&returnto=Python+%28programming+language%29 " title=") such as [ATmega 328P](/w/index.php?title=Special:CreateAccount&returnto=Python+%28programming+language%29 " title=")-based Arduino, as well as larger microcontrollers that are compatible with [MicroPython](/w/index.php?title=Special:UserLogin&returnto=Python+%28programming+language%29 "You are encouraged to create an account and log in; however, it is not mandatory"). Snek is an imperative language that (unlike Python) omits [object-oriented programming](/w/index.php?title=Special:UserLogin&returnto=Python+%28programming+language%29 " title="). Snek supports only one numeric data type, which features 32-bit [single precision](# "You're encouraged to log in; however, it's not mandatory. [o]") (resembling [JavaScript](# "Contents") numbers, though smaller).
- RustPython is an implementation written in [Rust](# "Contents") language. It aims to be compatible with CPython, including its C-ABI.[[158]](# "Contents") Currently, it is used in [GrepTimeDB](# "Contents") and [Ruff](# "Contents") among other projects.


### Unsupported implementations

[Stackless Python](# "Contents") is a significant fork of CPython that implements [microthreads](# "Contents"). This implementation uses the [call stack](#History "Contents") differently, thus allowing massively concurrent programs. PyPy also offers a stackless
version.[[159]](#Enhancement_Proposals "Contents")

Just-in-time Python compilers have been developed, but are now unsupported:

- Google began a project named [Unladen Swallow](#Enhancement_Proposals "Contents") in 2009: this project aimed to speed up the Python interpreter five-fold by using [LLVM](#Enhancement_Proposals "Contents"), and improve [multithreading](#Syntax_and_semantics "Contents") capability for scaling to thousands of cores,[[160]](#Indentation "Contents") while typical implementations are limited by the [global interpreter lock](#Indentation "Contents").
- [Psyco](#Indentation "Contents") is a discontinued [just-in-time](#Statements_and_control_flow "Contents") [specializing](#Statements_and_control_flow "Contents") compiler, which integrates with CPython and transforms bytecode to machine code at runtime. The emitted code is specialized for certain [data types](#Expressions "Contents") and is faster than standard Python code. Psyco does not support Python 2.7 or later.
- [PyS60](#Expressions "Contents") was a Python 2 interpreter for [Series 60](#Typing "Contents") mobile phones, which was released by [Nokia](#Arithmetic_operations "Contents") in 2005. The interpreter implemented many modules from Python's standard library, as well as additional modules for integration with the [Symbian](#Arithmetic_operations "Contents") operating system. The Nokia [N900](#Function_syntax "Contents") also supports Python through the [GTK](#Function_syntax "Contents") widget library, allowing programs to be written and run on the target device.[[161]](#Code_examples "Contents")


### Transpilers to other languages

There are several compilers/[transpilers](#Development_environments "Contents") to high-level object languages; the source language is unrestricted Python, a subset of Python, or a language similar to Python:

- Brython[[162]](#Implementations "Contents") and Transcrypt[[163]](#Reference_implementation "Contents")[[164]](#Limitations_of_the_reference_implementation "Contents") compile Python to [JavaScript](#Limitations_of_the_reference_implementation "Contents").
- [Cython](#Other_implementations "Contents") compiles a superset of Python to C. The resulting code can be used with Python via direct C-level API calls into the Python interpreter.
- PyJL compiles/transpiles a subset of Python to "human-readable, maintainable, and high-performance Julia source code".[[75]](#Transpilers_to_other_languages "Contents") Despite the developers' performance claims, this is not possible for *arbitrary* Python code; that is, compiling to a faster language or machine code is known to be impossible in the general case. The semantics of Python might potentially be changed, but in many cases speedup is possible with few or no changes in the Python code. The faster Julia source code can then be used from Python or compiled to machine code.
- [Nuitka](#Performance "Contents") compiles Python into C.[[165]](#Naming "Contents") This compiler works with Python 3.4 to 3.13 (and 2.6 and 2.7) for Python's main supported platforms (and Windows 7 or even Windows XP) and for Android. The compiler developers claim full support for Python 3.10, partial support for Python 3.11 and 3.12,  and experimental support for Python 3.13. Nuitka supports macOS including Apple Silicon-based versions.  The compiler is free of cost, though it has commercial add-ons (e.g., for hiding source code).
- [Numba](#References "Contents") is a JIT compiler that is used from Python; the compiler translates a subset of Python and NumPy code into fast machine code. This tool is enabled by adding a decorator to the relevant Python code.
- Pythran compiles a subset of Python 3 to C++ ([C++11](#Sources "Contents")).[[166]](#Further_reading "Contents")
- [RPython](#Further_reading "Contents") can be compiled to C, and it is used to build the PyPy interpreter for Python.
- The Python → 11l → C++ transpiler[[167]](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "Contents") compiles a subset of Python 3 to C++ ([C++17](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "vector-dropdown vector-page-titlebar-toc vector-button-flush-left")).

There are also specialized compilers:

- [MyHDL](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "button") is a Python-based [hardware description language](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "vector-dropdown-checkbox ") (HDL) that converts MyHDL code to [Verilog](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "vector-unpinned-container") or [VHDL](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "vector-unpinned-container") code.

Some older projects existed, as well as compilers not designed for use with Python 
3.x and related syntax:

- Google's Grumpy [transpiles](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "vector-unpinned-container") Python 2 to [Go](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "p-lang-btn").[[168]](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "Python (programmeertaal) – Afrikaans")[[169]](https://af.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeertaal) "Python (programmeertaal) – Afrikaans")[[170]](https://als.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(Programmiersprache) "Python (programmeertaal)") The latest release was in 2017.
- [IronPython](https://als.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(Programmiersprache) "Python (Programmiersprache) – Alemannic") allows running Python 2.7 programs with the .NET [Common Language Runtime](https://an.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Aragonese").[[171]](https://ar.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D8%A8%D8%A7%D9%8A%D8%AB%D9%88%D9%86_(%D9%84%D8%BA%D8%A9_%D8%A8%D8%B1%D9%85%D8%AC%D8%A9) "good article") An [alpha](ar "بايثون (لغة برمجة) – Arabic") version (released in 2021), is available for "Python 3.4, although features and behaviors from later versions may be included."[[172]](https://ast.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Asturian")
- [Jython](ast "Python – Asturian") compiles Python 2.7 to Java bytecode, allowing the use of Java libraries from a Python program.[[173]](https://azb.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D9%BE%D8%A7%DB%8C%D8%AA%D9%88%D9%86 "پایتون – South Azerbaijani")
- [Pyrex](azb "پایتون – South Azerbaijani") (last released in 2010) and [Shed Skin](https://ban.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Balinese") (last released in 2013) compile to C and C++ respectively.


### Performance

A performance comparison among various Python implementations, using a non-numerical 
(combinatorial) workload, was presented at EuroSciPy '13.[[174]](https://be.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(%D0%BC%D0%BE%D0%B2%D0%B0_%D0%BF%D1%80%D0%B0%D0%B3%D1%80%D0%B0%D0%BC%D0%B0%D0%B2%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%BD%D1%8F) "Python (мова праграмавання) – Belarusian") 
In addition, Python's performance relative to other programming languages is benchmarked 
by [The Computer Language Benchmarks Game](be "Python (мова праграмавання) –
Belarusian").[[175]](https://bg.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Bulgarian")

There are several approaches to optimizing Python performance, despite the inherent 
slowness of an [interpreted language](https://bh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E0%A4%AA%E0%A4%BE%E0%A4%87%E0%A4%A5%E0%A4%A8 "पाइथन – Bhojpuri"). These approaches include the following strategies or tools:

- [Just-in-time compilation](https://bn.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E0%A6%AA%E0%A6%BE%E0%A6%87%E0%A6%A5%E0%A6%A8_(%E0%A6%AA%E0%A7%8D%E0%A6%B0%E0%A7%8B%E0%A6%97%E0%A7%8D%E0%A6%B0%E0%A6%BE%E0%A6%AE%E0%A6%BF%E0%A6%82_%E0%A6%AD%E0%A6%BE%E0%A6%B7%E0%A6%BE) "পাইথন (প্রোগ্রামিং ভাষা) – Bangla"): Dynamically compiling parts of a Python program during the execution of the program. This technique is used in libraries such as [Numba](bn "পাইথন (প্রোগ্রামিং ভাষা) – Bangla") and [PyPy](bn "পাইথন (প্রোগ্রামিং ভাষা)").
- [Static compilation](https://br.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(lavar_programmi%C3%B1) "Python (lavar programmiñ) – Breton"): Sometimes, Python code can be compiled into machine code sometime before execution. An example of this approach is [Cython](https://br.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(lavar_programmi%C3%B1) "Python (lavar programmiñ) – Breton"), which compiles Python into C.
- [Concurrency](br "Python (lavar programmiñ)") and [parallelism](https://bs.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programski_jezik) "Python (programski jezik) – Bosnian"): Multiple tasks can be run simultaneously. Python contains modules such as \`multiprocessing\` to support this form of parallelism. Moreover, this approach helps to overcome limitations of the [Global Interpreter Lock](https://bug.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Buginese") (GIL) in CPU tasks.
- Efficient [data structures](bug "Python"): Performance can also be improved by using data types such as `Set` for membership tests, or `deque` from `collections` for [queue](https://ceb.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programming_language) "Python") operations.
- Performance gains can be observed by utilizing libraries such as [NumPy](ceb "Python (programming language) – Cebuano"). Most high performance Python libraries use [C](https://ckb.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D9%BE%D8%A7%DB%8C%D8%AA%DB%86%D9%86_(%D8%B2%D9%85%D8%A7%D9%86%DB%8C_%D8%A8%DB%95%D8%B1%D9%86%D8%A7%D9%85%DB%95%D8%B3%D8%A7%D8%B2%DB%8C) "Python (programming language)") or [Fortran](https://ckb.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D9%BE%D8%A7%DB%8C%D8%AA%DB%86%D9%86_(%D8%B2%D9%85%D8%A7%D9%86%DB%8C_%D8%A8%DB%95%D8%B1%D9%86%D8%A7%D9%85%DB%95%D8%B3%D8%A7%D8%B2%DB%8C) "پایتۆن (زمانی بەرنامەسازی) – Central Kurdish") under the hood instead of the Python interpreter.[[176]](https://cs.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Czech")


## Language development

Python's development is conducted mostly through the *Python Enhancement Proposal* 
(PEP) process; this process is the primary mechanism for proposing major new features, 
collecting community input on issues, and documenting Python design decisions.[[177]](da "Python (programmeringssprog) – Danish") 
Python coding style is covered in PEP 8.[[86]](de "Python (Programmiersprache)") 
Outstanding PEPs are reviewed and commented on by the Python community and the steering 
council.[[177]](https://el.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Greek")

Enhancement of the language corresponds with development of the CPython reference 
implementation. The mailing list python-dev is the primary forum for the language's 
development. Specific issues were originally discussed in the [Roundup](eo "Python (programlingvo) – Esperanto") [bug tracker](https://es.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Spanish") hosted by the
foundation.[[178]](https://et.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmeerimiskeel) "Python (programmeerimiskeel) – Estonian") 
In 2022, all issues and discussions were migrated to [GitHub](et "Python
(programmeerimiskeel)").[[179]](https://fa.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D9%BE%D8%A7%DB%8C%D8%AA%D9%88%D9%86_(%D8%B2%D8%A8%D8%A7%D9%86_%D8%A8%D8%B1%D9%86%D8%A7%D9%85%D9%87%E2%80%8C%D9%86%D9%88%DB%8C%D8%B3%DB%8C) "Python (informatika)") 
Development originally took place on a [self-hosted](https://fa.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D9%BE%D8%A7%DB%8C%D8%AA%D9%88%D9%86_(%D8%B2%D8%A8%D8%A7%D9%86_%D8%A8%D8%B1%D9%86%D8%A7%D9%85%D9%87%E2%80%8C%D9%86%D9%88%DB%8C%D8%B3%DB%8C) "پایتون (زبان برنامه‌نویسی) – Persian") source-code repository running [Mercurial](fa "پایتون (زبان برنامه‌نویسی)"), until Python moved to GitHub in January
2017.[[180]](https://fj.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Fijian")

CPython's public releases have three types, distinguished by which part of the version 
number is incremented:

- *Backward-incompatible versions*, where code is expected to break and must be manually [ported](https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(langage) "Python (langage) – French"). The first part of the version number is incremented. These releases happen infrequently—version 3.0 was released 8 years after 2.0. According to Guido van Rossum, a version 4.0 will probably never exist.[[181]](https://glk.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D9%BE%D8%A7%D9%8A%D8%AA%D8%A4%D9%86_(%D8%A8%D8%B1%D9%86%D8%A7%D9%85%D9%87%E2%80%8C%D9%86%D9%8A%D9%88%D9%8A%D8%B3%D9%8A_%D8%B2%D9%88%D8%A7%D9%86) "پايتؤن (برنامه‌نيويسي زوان) – Gilaki")
- *Major or "feature" releases* are largely compatible with the previous version but introduce new features. The second part of the version number is incremented. Starting with Python 3.9, these releases are expected to occur annually.[[182]](gu "પાયથોન (પ્રોગ્રામિંગ ભાષા) – Gujarati")[[183]](https://ha.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_programming_language "Python programming language – Hausa") Each major version is supported by bug fixes for several years after its release.[[184]](he "פייתון – Hebrew")
- *Bug fix releases*,[[185]](https://hr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programski_jezik) "Python (programski jezik) – Croatian") which introduce no new features, occur approximately every three months; these releases are made when a sufficient number of bugs have been fixed [upstream](https://hu.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programoz%C3%A1si_nyelv) "Python (programski jezik)") since the last release. Security vulnerabilities are also patched in these releases. The third and final part of the version number is incremented.[[185]](https://hy.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Armenian")

Many [alpha, beta, and release-candidates](https://ia.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(linguage_de_programmation) "Python (linguage de programmation) – Interlingua") are also released as previews and for testing before final releases. Although there is a rough schedule for releases, they are often delayed if the code is not ready yet. Python's development team monitors the state of the code by running a large [unit test](https://id.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(bahasa_pemrograman) "Python (bahasa pemrograman) – Indonesian") suite during
development.[[186]](https://io.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Ido")

The major [academic conference](io "Python") on Python is [PyCon](https://is.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(forritunarm%C3%A1l) "Python (forritunarmál) – Icelandic"). Also, there are special Python mentoring programs, such as [PyLadies](is "Python (forritunarmál)").


## Naming

Python's name is inspired by the British comedy group [Monty Python](it "Python"), whom Python creator Guido van Rossum enjoyed while developing the language. Monty Python references appear frequently in Python code and
culture;[[187]](https://jbo.wikipedia.org/wiki/paiton "paiton – Lojban") for example, 
the [metasyntactic variables](https://ka.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E1%83%9E%E1%83%90%E1%83%98%E1%83%97%E1%83%9D%E1%83%9C%E1%83%98_(%E1%83%9E%E1%83%A0%E1%83%9D%E1%83%92%E1%83%A0%E1%83%90%E1%83%9B%E1%83%98%E1%83%A0%E1%83%94%E1%83%91%E1%83%98%E1%83%A1_%E1%83%94%E1%83%9C%E1%83%90) "პაითონი (პროგრამირების ენა) – Georgian") often used in Python literature are
[*spam* and *eggs*](ka "პაითონი (პროგრამირების ენა) – Georgian"), rather than the traditional
[*foo* and *bar*](ka "პაითონი (პროგრამირების
ენა)").[[187]](https://kaa.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programmalast%C4%B1r%C4%B1w_tili) "Python (programmalastırıw tili) –
Kara-Kalpak")[[188]](https://kk.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Kazakh") Also, 
the official Python documentation contains various references to Monty Python routines.[[189]](https://ks.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D9%BE%D8%A7%DB%8C%D8%AA%DA%BE%D9%8E%D9%86_(%D9%BE%D8%B1%D9%9B%D9%88%DA%AF%D8%B1%D8%A7%D9%85%D9%90%D9%86%D9%9B%DA%AF_%D8%B2%D9%8E%D8%A8%D8%A7%D9%86) "پایتھَن (پرٛوگرامِنٛگ زَبان) –
Kashmiri")[[190]](https://ku.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(ziman%C3%AA_bernamesaziy%C3%AA) "Python (zimanê bernamesaziyê) – Kurdish") 
Python users are sometimes referred to as "Pythonistas".[[191]](ky "Python – Kyrgyz")


## Languages influenced by Python

- [Cobra](https://lmo.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Lombard") has an *Acknowledgements* document that lists Python first among influencing languages.[[192]](https://lo.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E0%BB%84%E0%BA%9E%E0%BA%97%E0%BA%AD%E0%BA%99_(%E0%BA%9E%E0%BA%B2%E0%BA%AA%E0%BA%B2%E0%BB%82%E0%BA%9B%E0%BA%A3%E0%BB%81%E0%BA%81%E0%BA%A3%E0%BA%A1) "ໄພທອນ (ພາສາໂປຣແກຣມ) – Lao")
- [ECMAScript](lo "ໄພທອນ (ພາສາໂປຣແກຣມ)") and [JavaScript](https://lt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Lithuanian") borrowed iterators and [generators](https://lt.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Lithuanian") from Python.[[193]](lv "Python (programmēšanas valoda) – Latvian")
- [Go](https://mk.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%9F%D0%B0%D1%98%D1%82%D0%BE%D0%BD_(%D0%BF%D1%80%D0%BE%D0%B3%D1%80%D0%B0%D0%BC%D1%81%D0%BA%D0%B8_%D1%98%D0%B0%D0%B7%D0%B8%D0%BA) "Пајтон (програмски јазик) – Macedonian") is designed for "speed of working in a dynamic language like Python".[[194]](https://ml.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E0%B4%AA%E0%B5%88%E0%B4%A4%E0%B5%8D%E0%B4%A4%E0%B5%BA_(%E0%B4%AA%E0%B5%8D%E0%B4%B0%E0%B5%8B%E0%B4%97%E0%B5%8D%E0%B4%B0%E0%B4%BE%E0%B4%AE%E0%B4%BF%E0%B4%99%E0%B5%8D%E0%B4%99%E0%B5%8D_%E0%B4%AD%E0%B4%BE%E0%B4%B7) "പൈത്തൺ (പ്രോഗ്രാമിങ്ങ് ഭാഷ) – Malayalam")
- [Julia](https://ml.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E0%B4%AA%E0%B5%88%E0%B4%A4%E0%B5%8D%E0%B4%A4%E0%B5%BA_(%E0%B4%AA%E0%B5%8D%E0%B4%B0%E0%B5%8B%E0%B4%97%E0%B5%8D%E0%B4%B0%E0%B4%BE%E0%B4%AE%E0%B4%BF%E0%B4%99%E0%B5%8D%E0%B4%99%E0%B5%8D_%E0%B4%AD%E0%B4%BE%E0%B4%B7) "പൈത്തൺ (പ്രോഗ്രാമിങ്ങ് ഭാഷ) – Malayalam") was designed to be "as usable for general programming as Python".[[33]](https://mn.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Mongolian")
- [Mojo](https://mn.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python "Python – Mongolian") is almost[[34]](/w/load.php?lang=en&modules=ext.cite.parsoid.styles%7Cext.cite.styles%7Cext.pygments%2CwikimediaBadges%7Cext.uls.interlanguage%7Cext.visualEditor.desktopArticleTarget.noscript%7Cext.wikimediaBadges.ulsV2%7Cext.wikimediamessages.styles%7Cjquery.makeCollapsible.styles%7Cmediawiki.skinning.content.parsoid%7Cskins.vector.icons%2Cstyles%7Cskins.vector.search.codex.styles%7Cwikibase.client.init&only=styles&skin=vector-2022 [↑](/wiki/Wikipedia:About "Learn about Wikipedia and how it works") 3. **Tier 1**: 64-bit [Linux](/wiki/Help:Contents "Guidance on how to use and edit Wikipedia"), [macOS](/wiki/Help:Contents "Guidance on how to use and edit Wikipedia"); 64- and 32-bit [Windows](/wiki/Help:Contents "Guidance on how to use and edit Wikipedia")[[6]](/wiki/Wikipedia:Community_portal "The hub for editors")
4. **Tier 2**: E.g. 32-bit [WebAssembly](/wiki/Wikipedia:File_upload_wizard "A list of recent changes to Wikipedia [r]") (WASI)
5. **Tier 3**: 64-bit [Android](/wiki/Special:SpecialPages "Add images or other media for use on Wikipedia"),[[7]](/wiki/Special:Search "Search Wikipedia [f]") [iOS](/wiki/Special:Search "Search Wikipedia [f]"), [FreeBSD](/wiki/Special:Search "Search Wikipedia [f]"), and (32-bit) [Raspberry Pi OS](/wiki/Special:Search "Search Wikipedia [f]")

7. [↑](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en " title=") 
`del` in Python does not behave the same way `delete` in languages such as [C++](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en " title=") does, where such a word is used to call the [destructor](https://donate.wikimedia.org/?wmf_source=donate&wmf_medium=sidebar&wmf_campaign=en.wikipedia.org&uselang=en " title=") and deallocate heap memory.

Mojo as a member of the Python family [..] Embracing Python massively simplifies our design efforts, because most of the syntax is already specified.  [..] we decided that the right long-term goal for Mojo is to provide a superset of Python (i.e. be compatible with existing programs) and to embrace the CPython immediately for long-tail ecosystem enablement. To a Python programmer, we expect and hope that Mojo will be immediately familiar, while also providing new tools for developing systems-level code that enable you to do things that Python falls back to C and C++ for.*

[ISBN](lmo "Python") [978-1-4920-5632-4](https://lo.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E0%BB%84%E0%BA%9E%E0%BA%97%E0%BA%AD%E0%BA%99_(%E0%BA%9E%E0%BA%B2%E0%BA%AA%E0%BA%B2%E0%BB%82%E0%BA%9B%E0%BA%A3%E0%BB%81%E0%BA%81%E0%BA%A3%E0%BA%A1) "ໄພທອນ (ພາສາໂປຣແກຣມ) – Lao").*
